1. 18th BRICS Summit 2026
The 18th BRICS Summit was held in New Delhi, India, on 12–13 September 2026, under India’s chairship.
India’s theme for the year was: “Building for Resilience, Innovation, Cooperation and Sustainability”
The theme reflects India’s focus on development, sustainability, innovation and the interests of the
Global South.
What is BRICS?
BRICS is a grouping of major emerging economies that cooperate on issues such as:
Economy and trade
Finance
Energy
Food security
Climate change
Technology
Health
Global governance
The original grouping was BRIC — Brazil, Russia, India and China.
South Africa joined in 2010, making it BRICS.
The grouping has now expanded significantly.
BRICS Members in 2026
There are 11 full members:
Country Country
Brazil Russia
India China
South Africa Egypt
Ethiopia Iran
United Arab Emirates Indonesia
Saudi Arabia
According to the Government of India, these 11 countries together account for about 49.5% of the world's
population, 40% of global GDP and 26% of global trade.

2026 BRICS Summit — Key Highlights
New Delhi Declaration: Focused on political-security, economic-financial, and cultural cooperation
and a fairer global order.
Global Governance Reform: BRICS called for greater representation of emerging economies in the UN,
IMF and World Bank.
Local Currencies & Payments: Members discussed easier cross-border payments, greater use of
local currencies and CBDC cooperation.
Artificial Intelligence: China proposed a BRICS AI Open Source Zone to promote AI cooperation.
Food & Energy Security: Cooperation discussed on food, energy, critical minerals, supply chains and
climate change.
Health: India announced an early warning system for infectious diseases.
Global Conflicts: BRICS discussed West Asia, Iran, Ukraine and Sudan, stressing dialogue and
diplomacy.
Why is BRICS Important for India?
Global South Cooperation: Helps India work with developing and emerging economies on common
issues.
UN Reforms: Supports India's demand for a more representative global governance system.
Payment Diversification: Encourages local currencies and alternative payment systems, reducing
dependence on a single system.
Economic Cooperation: Promotes trade, investment, infrastructure and technology.
Diplomatic Influence: Gives India a platform to engage with major countries such as China, Russia,
Iran, UAE, Saudi Arabia and Brazil.
India as BRICS Chair in 2026
Fourth BRICS Chairship for India.
Previous chairships: 2012, 2016 and 2021.
2026 marks 20 years of BRICS since its establishment.

NABARD  CURRENT  AFFAIRS
Agriculture
1. Grow Indigo Pays Farmers for Agricultural Carbon Credits
Source: Business Standard
Context
Agri-tech startup Grow Indigo has paid around 2,500 farmers ₹3,000–₹15,000 each for more than
57,000 agricultural carbon credits. The company made the payment from its own funds, even though
the carbon credits have not yet been completely sold to buyers.
Key Highlights
Direct-Seeded Rice
Many carbon credits were generated through Direct-Seeded Rice (DSR) instead of traditional rice
transplantation.
DSR: Rice seeds are directly sown in the field, avoiding the need to first grow seedlings in a nursery and
then transplant them.
DSR Can Reduce Methane Emissions
A study by CSK Himachal Pradesh Krishi Vishvavidyalaya (CSKHPKV) and Savannah Seeds found that
direct-seeded rice using SAVA-134 FullPage:
Reduced methane emissions by nearly 90%.
Improved water productivity.
Improved soil health.
Increased potential for carbon-credit generation.
Key Terms
Carbon Credit: A tradable certificate representing the reduction or removal of a specified amount of
greenhouse-gas emissions, generally 1 tonne of CO₂-equivalent.
Agricultural Carbon Credit: Carbon credits generated through farming practices that reduce
emissions or increase carbon storage.
Direct-Seeded Rice (DSR): A method in which rice seeds are directly sown in the field instead of
transplanting seedlings.
Methane (CH₄): A powerful greenhouse gas produced in large quantities in flooded rice fields.
Carbon Market: A market where carbon credits are generated, bought and sold.
Water Productivity: The amount of agricultural output produced per unit of water used.
2. Grass Mache Technique
Source: New Indian Express

Context A Srinagar artisan developed the Grass Mache technique, converting aquatic weeds and
grasses removed from Dal Lake into decorative and utility products.
What It Is
An innovative craft technique using discarded aquatic weeds and grasses from Dal Lake as raw

Adapts techniques from Kashmir's centuries-old papier-mâché tradition
Creating sustainable products from biological waste
How It Works
Dried weeds are processed into a fine fibrous pulp
Mixed with suitable materials
Moulded into shapes and dried
Producing a natural, stone-like texture
Important Facts for Exams
Grass Mache — craft technique using Dal Lake aquatic weeds, adapted from papier-mâché
Dal Lake, Srinagar — an urban lake suffering from eutrophication, encroachment and weed
infestation
Main invasive weeds in Dal Lake: Azolla, Salvinia, water hyacinth and Ceratophyllum
Eutrophication — nutrient enrichment causing excessive weed and algal growth, depleting dissolved
oxygen
Dal Lake is managed by the Lakes Conservation and Management Authority (LCMA), formerly LAWDA
Kashmiri papier-mâché holds a GI tag; other J&K GIs include Kashmir Pashmina, Kashmir Saffron,
Kashmir Walnut Wood Carving and Basohli Painting
Wular Lake in J&K is a Ramsar Site; Hokersar, Surinsar-Mansar and Tsomoriri are others in the
region
Related concept: circular economy — converting waste streams into inputs

4. Ophiopogon japonicus Recorded for the First Time in India
Ophiopogon japonicus, an evergreen perennial herb, was recorded for the first time in India from
West Kameng district, Arunachal Pradesh.
It belongs to the Asparagaceae family and is commonly known as dwarf lilyturf or mondo grass.
The species is mainly found in China, Japan and Korea, generally at elevations of 200–2,800 metres.
Its roots, known as Ophiopogonis Radix (Maidong), are used in traditional East Asian medicine.

2. Tata Steel Commissions COG Injection Project in Odisha
Tata Steel commissioned India’s first Coke Oven Gas (COG) Injection Project at Blast Furnace 1 of its
Meramandali plant in Odisha.
COG is a hydrogen-rich by-product of coke production and contains more than 55% hydrogen.
The purified gas is injected into blast furnaces through tuyeres, reducing dependence on carbon-
based reducing agents.
The project aims to reduce fossil-fuel consumption and carbon emissions and support Tata Steel’s
Net Zero target for 2045.

The awards recognised his contributions to governance, infrastructure development and regional
growth.
5. N. Purushothama Mallya Passes Away
N. Purushothama Mallya, a noted educationist, writer and Konkani language activist, passed away
at the age of 97.
He received the Padma Shri in 2015 for Literature and Education.
He contributed to Konkani literature and translated Thirukkural into Konkani.
He also received the Sahitya Akademi Translation Award in 2008.
6. World Suicide Prevention Day 2026
World Suicide Prevention Day is observed annually on 10 September to raise awareness and reduce
stigma around suicide.
The 2024–26 theme is “Changing the Narrative on Suicide”, with the call to action “Start the
Conversation.”
The day was established in 2003 by the International Association for Suicide Prevention in
collaboration with WHO.
The first observance was held on 10 September 2003 in Stockholm, Sweden.
7. National Forest Martyrs Day 2026
National Forest Martyrs Day is observed in India on 11 September to honour people who lost their lives
protecting forests and wildlife.
The day commemorates the Khejarli Massacre of 1730 in Rajasthan.
The observance was designated by the Ministry of Environment, Forest and Climate Change in 2013.
The day is commemorated at the National Forest Martyrs Memorial in Dehradun.
8. International Day for South-South Cooperation 2026
The International Day for South-South Cooperation is observed annually on 12 September.
The 2026 theme is “Solidarity Rising: Global Alliance for Inclusive and Innovative Development.”
It promotes cooperation among developing countries in economic, social, cultural, technical and
political fields.

Agriculture
1. Badri Cattle
Source: Global Agriculture | Subject: Species in News / Science and Technology
Context India produced its first Badri calf through OPU-IVF technology at Pantnagar — a milestone for
indigenous cattle conservation.
What It Is
An indigenous humped cattle (Bos indicus) breed native to the Himalayan region of Uttarakhand
Locally called Pahadi
Named after the holy shrine of Badrinath
The first cattle breed from Uttarakhand to be officially certified and registered by ICAR's NBAGR
Recognised as the state animal of Uttarakhand
Habitat
Found across the hilly and mountainous tracts of Uttarakhand
Particularly in the Garhwal and Kumaon divisions — Almora, Pauri Garhwal, Chamoli and
Champawat
Thrives in high-altitude environments, uneven slopes and cold climates where exotic or plains-bred
cattle struggle to adapt
Important Facts for Exams
Badri cattle — Bos indicus, indigenous to Uttarakhand; locally Pahadi; the state animal
Named after the Badrinath shrine
The first Uttarakhand cattle breed registered by NBAGR
Found in Garhwal and Kumaon — Almora, Pauri Garhwal, Chamoli, Champawat
Classified as a dual-purpose breed
NBAGR — National Bureau of Animal Genetic Resources, Karnal, the nodal body for breed registration
Bos indicus — humped, zebu cattle of the tropics; Bos taurus — humpless European cattle
The first Badri calf through OPU-IVF was born 11 September 2026, from a Sahiwal surrogate, in a
collaboration between ICAR-NDRI Karnal and GBPUAT Pantnagar, funded by the Uttarakhand
Biotechnology Council, Haldi
Cloned Badri embryos have also been generated, with cloning of elite cows under exploration
2. PM Kisan Maandhan Yojana Completes Seven Years
Source: PIB

September 2026 full compilation  ·  Page 107 of 191
“The exam rewards the one who came back to the page.”
WhatsApp channel: whatsapp.com/channel/0029Vao03aUADTO9sOoigE2F  ·  +91 87086 52887  ·  c4scourses.in  ·  free, share it on
Context On 12 September 2026, the Pradhan Mantri Kisan Maandhan Yojana (PM-KMY) completed
seven years — a voluntary contributory pension scheme for the old-age social security of Small and
Marginal Farmers (SMFs).
The Scheme
Launched in 2019 by the Ministry of Agriculture and Farmers Welfare
Implemented with the Life Insurance Corporation of India (LIC) as the pension fund manager
A voluntary, contributory old-age pension scheme
Offers eligible landholding SMFs a steady monthly pension from the age of 60
Eligibility
Small and marginal farmers with cultivable landholding up to 2 hectares
Aged 18 to 40 years
Name appearing in the land records of the respective State or UT as on 1 August 2019
Important Facts for Exams
PM-KMY launched 2019; completed seven years on 12 September 2026
Implemented by the Ministry of Agriculture and Farmers Welfare with LIC
Eligibility: landholding up to 2 hectares, age 18–40, land records as on 1 August 2019
Contribution ₹55–₹200 per month, age-based
Assured pension of ₹3,000 per month on attaining 60 years
The Central Government makes an equal matching contribution to the pension fund
On the pensioner's death, the spouse receives 50 per cent as family pension
Farmers may opt to have contributions deducted from their PM-KISAN benefits
3. GOBARdhan Scheme Guidelines Rolled Out
Source: Business Standard
Context The government notified detailed operational guidelines for the ₹23,731 crore GOBARdhan
scheme, to develop India's compressed biogas (CBG) sector — assuring full offtake and introducing a
pricing mechanism. The Cabinet approved the scheme last month.
What is GOBARdhan Scheme
GOBARdhan stands for Galvanizing Organic Bio-Agro Resources Dhan. It is a Government of India
initiative to convert cattle dung, agricultural waste, kitchen waste and other biodegradable waste into
useful products such as biogas, Compressed Biogas (CBG) and organic manure.
About
Launched: 2018
Ministry: Ministry of Jal Shakti
Nodal Department: Department of Drinking Water and Sanitation (DDWS)
Initially launched under: Swachh Bharat Mission–Grameen (SBM-G)
Main idea: Waste to Wealth
It promotes a circular economy, where waste is converted into energy and useful agricultural inputs.
How GOBARdhan Works
Organic waste → Biogas/CBG + Bio-slurry → Energy + Organic manure
For example:
A village collects cattle dung and crop residue → puts it into a biogas plant → produces biogas for

September 2026 full compilation  ·  Page 108 of 191
“The exam rewards the one who came back to the page.”
WhatsApp channel: whatsapp.com/channel/0029Vao03aUADTO9sOoigE2F  ·  +91 87086 52887  ·  c4scourses.in  ·  free, share it on
energy → the remaining bio-slurry can be used as organic manure.
This helps farmers earn additional income while reducing waste and pollution.
4. 9th ASEAN–India Ministerial Meeting on Agriculture and Forestry
The 9th ASEAN–India Ministerial Meeting on Agriculture and Forestry (AIMMAF) was held in New
Delhi on 9 September 2026.
India and ASEAN reaffirmed cooperation for a food-secure, sustainable and climate-resilient
agricultural sector.
Key areas include climate-resilient agriculture, low-emission rice, water productivity, AI-enabled
digital agriculture, soil health and agroforestry.
India–ASEAN trade in goods reached USD 128 billion in FY2025–26.
5. Dryland Congress 2026 and Delhi Declaration on Drylands
Dryland Congress 2026 concluded in New Delhi with the Delhi Declaration on Drylands (3D).
The Congress focused on sustainable dryland agriculture and stronger South–South and triangular
cooperation.
It was organised by ICAR and ICRISAT under the theme “Transforming Dryland Agriculture with
South–South Cooperation.”
The event also marked 50 years of the ICAR–ICRISAT partnership.
6. Mangrove GIS Portal Launched in Gujarat
Union Minister Bhupender Yadav launched the Mangrove Geographic Information System (MGIS)
Portal in Bhuj, Gujarat.
The portal supports habitat monitoring, conservation planning and technology-based mangrove
restoration.
Gujarat has restored 27,652 hectares of mangroves under MISHTI, against the 2023–27 target of
29,250 hectares.
The Guneri Inland Mangrove ecosystem was highlighted as an important community-led
conservation initiative.
7. New Amphibian Species ‘Gegeneophis chandgadi’ Discovered
Scientists discovered a new subterranean, legless amphibian species, Gegeneophis chandgadi, in
Chandgad, Kolhapur, Maharashtra.
It is a caecilian, a group of worm-like amphibians adapted mainly to underground life.
The species was found in residential and agricultural landscapes near human habitation.
The discovery takes the number of Gegeneophis species recorded in India to 12, with 11 endemic to the
Western Ghats.

RBI  CURRENT  AFFAIRS
Banking and Finance
1. MDR Framework for UPI Transactions
Source: PIB
Context A Merchant Discount Rate (MDR) framework for UPI has been introduced, effective 15 October
2026, applying to merchant transactions with specified exemptions.
What MDR Is
A fee charged within the merchant payment ecosystem for processing digital transactions
Applies to certain Person-to-Merchant (P2M) transactions
Shared among banks, payment service providers and UPI app providers
It is not a tax or government levy, and customers are not required to pay it

September 2026 full compilation  ·  Page 110 of 191
“The exam rewards the one who came back to the page.”
WhatsApp channel: whatsapp.com/channel/0029Vao03aUADTO9sOoigE2F  ·  +91 87086 52887  ·  c4scourses.in  ·  free, share it on
Introduced under the Payment and Settlement Systems Act, 2007
The Fee Structure
Category Rate
All P2P transactions Zero, irrespective of value
P2M above ₹2,000 0.4 per cent
Transactions above ₹75,000 Capped at ₹300
Essential sectors — railways, telecom, fuel, insurance, utility bills, agricultural
inputs
Flat ₹5 for payments over ₹2,000
Capital market — mutual funds, securities, stockbrokers 0.02 per cent, capped at ₹300
No charges on payments up to ₹2,000 through RuPay debit cards either
Exemptions
All P2P transactions — zero MDR
P2M up to ₹2,000 — zero MDR
Small merchants under P2PM — zero MDR up to ₹1 lakh monthly receipts
UPI AutoPay and Mandates — including recurring utility bills, subscriptions and investments
Financial Inclusion Provision
5 per cent of collections will fund UPI adoption among small merchants
About UPI
A real-time payment system enabling 24/7 instant, interoperable and secure transactions
The world's largest retail fast payment system by transaction volume
Developed by the NPCI
Key features: two-factor authentication, open API, single app integration across multiple bank
accounts
International Acceptance
UPI is accepted in 11 countries: Greece, Maldives, UAE, Singapore, Bhutan, Nepal, Sri Lanka, France,
Mauritius, Qatar and Cambodia
2. EPFO Wage Ceiling Raised to ₹25,000
The Union Cabinet approved an increase in the EPFO wage ceiling from ₹15,000 to ₹25,000 per month,
effective 17 September 2026.
More than 51 lakh additional employees are expected to come under mandatory EPFO coverage.
The government is expected to spend around ₹11,339 crore annually, with approximately ₹56,696
crore over five years.
The expanded coverage relates to EPF, Employees’ Pension Scheme (EPS) and Employees’ Deposit
Linked Insurance (EDLI), subject to applicable provisions.
3. RBI Absorbs ₹3.93 Trillion Through VRRR
RBI absorbed ₹3,93,352 crore through a Variable Rate Reverse Repo (VRRR) auction against the
notified ₹5 trillion.
The cut-off rate was 5.24%, while surplus liquidity in the banking system was around ₹10.73 trillion on 11
September.
RBI also announced ₹1 trillion of Open Market Operation (OMO) sales in three tranches.

Important Facts for Exams
Global Gender Gap Index — published by the World Economic Forum, first in 2006; headquartered at
Cologny, Geneva
India's rank: 131; parity score 64.5 per cent
Iceland has topped the index for over a decade
The index measures parity across four sub-indices:
Economic Participation and Opportunity
Educational Attainment
Health and Survival
Political Empowerment
India has historically scored well on Political Empowerment but poorly on Economic Participation
and Health and Survival — particularly on sex ratio at birth
The index measures gaps between women and men, not absolute levels — so a poor country with
equal outcomes can rank above a rich one with unequal outcomes
A score of 1 = full parity; 0 = complete imparity
Other WEF publications: the Global Risks Report, Global Competitiveness Report, Travel and Tourism
Development Index, and the Future of Jobs Report
Related Indian data: female labour force participation 41.7 per cent (2023-24); women hold 55.7 per
cent of Jan Dhan accounts; women are over one-third of the workforce in 237 districts (ASUSE 2025)

Tons River.

benefits.

institutions.

The 2026 theme is “Global action for a cooler planet.”
The date marks the signing of the Montreal Protocol in 1987.
The UN declared 16 September as the International Day for the Preservation of the Ozone Layer in 1994.

4. ‘Ophiopogon japonicus’ Recorded in India for the First Time
Researchers from the Wildlife Institute of India (WII) and the Uluberia Botanical Institute Herbarium
recorded Ophiopogon japonicus for the first time in India.
The species was found in West Kameng district, Arunachal Pradesh.
It is an evergreen perennial herb of the Asparagaceae family, commonly called dwarf lilyturf or
mondo grass.
Its roots are traditionally used in East Asian medicine.

11. Princess Astrid of Norway Passes Away
Princess Astrid Maud Ingeborg of Norway passed away at the age of 94 on 11 September 2026.

She served as First Lady of Norway from 1954 to 1968.
She continued to undertake official and humanitarian duties for the Norwegian Royal Family for
several decades.

1. The Cheetah and Project Cheetah
Source: ThePrint
Context A four-year status review of Project Cheetah released by Kuno National Park shows India's
cheetah population has risen to 52, including 32 cubs born on Indian soil. The programme has entered
the consolidation phase.
About the Cheetah
Acinonyx jubatus — a large felid of the family Felidae
The sole extant member of the genus Acinonyx
The fastest living terrestrial animal, accelerating from 0 to 100 km/h in about three seconds
Conservation Status
IUCN: Vulnerable globally; the Asiatic subspecies is Critically Endangered
CITES: Appendix I
Important Facts for Exams
Cheetah population: 52 — 49 at Kuno, 3 at Gandhi Sagar; 32 cubs born in India
17 free-ranging at Kuno; the programme is in its consolidation phase
Acinonyx jubatus; sole member of genus Acinonyx
IUCN Vulnerable; Asiatic subspecies Critically Endangered; CITES Appendix I
Declared extinct in India in 1952; the last were reportedly shot in Koriya, present-day Chhattisgarh
Project Cheetah launched 17 September 2022 at Kuno, with 8 cheetahs from Namibia; 12 more from
South Africa in February 2023
The world's first intercontinental large carnivore translocation
Kuno is in Sheopur district, and Gandhi Sagar in Mandsaur-Neemuch, both in Madhya Pradesh
The cheetah is the only large carnivore to have gone extinct in independent India
2. Gegeneophis chandgadi
Source: TH
Context Scientists from the Zoological Survey of India and Savitribai Phule Pune University described a
new limbless amphibian, Gegeneophis chandgadi, discovered in backyard leaf litter in Buzawade
village, Maharashtra's Western Ghats.
What It Is
A subterranean, limbless amphibian — a caecilian
Family Grandisoniidae; genus Gegeneophis, commonly called blind caecilians

September 2026 full compilation  ·  Page 119 of 191
“Study the question paper, not just the syllabus.”
WhatsApp channel: whatsapp.com/channel/0029Vao03aUADTO9sOoigE2F  ·  +91 87086 52887  ·  c4scourses.in  ·  free, share it on
An annulocylix caecilian — slender, ringed and cylindrical, without a tail
Where Found
Buzawade village, Chandgad taluka, Kolhapur district
In the northern Western Ghats of Maharashtra
Habitat
Highly fossorial — spends nearly its entire life underground
Inhabits human-modified rural agroecosystems and village backyards
Thrives beneath decaying wood piles, compost and organic waste, and moist bamboo leaf litter
Where rich organic matter is conserved and synthetic chemical pesticides are avoided
Important Facts for Exams
Gegeneophis chandgadi — a caecilian, family Grandisoniidae
Found at Buzawade village, Chandgad taluka, Kolhapur district, northern Western Ghats
Described by the Zoological Survey of India and Savitribai Phule Pune University
Caecilians form the order Gymnophiona — the third order of amphibians, alongside Anura (frogs
and toads) and Caudata/Urodela (salamanders and newts)
They are limbless, burrowing amphibians with vestigial eyes and chemosensory tentacles
Fossorial — adapted to digging and living underground
Annuli — the ring-like folds encircling the body
Notable: the species was found in a human-modified backyard, not pristine forest — showing
agroecosystems free of pesticides can hold undescribed biodiversity
The Western Ghats is a UNESCO World Heritage Site and one of the world's eight hottest biodiversity
hotspots
ZSI — Zoological Survey of India, established 1916, headquartered at Kolkata, under the MoEFCC

Social Issue
1. Article 371 and Asymmetric Federalism
Source: Indian Express
Context The Ministry of Home Affairs reached an in-principle understanding with the Apex Body, Leh
(ABL) and the Kargil Democratic Alliance (KDA) to propose inserting a new Article 371K into the
Constitution.
What Asymmetric Federalism Means
Part XXI of the Constitution — Temporary, Transitional and Special Provisions
Embodies the constitutional flexibility to grant differentiated political, cultural, economic and
administrative protections to specific regions
Based on unique historical, ethnic or developmental circumstances
How Article 371 Evolved
Original purpose (1950) — not for cultural or tribal autonomy. It was a transitional administrative
mechanism placing erstwhile princely states (Part B States such as Mysore, Hyderabad and
Rajasthan) under Presidential supervision for 10 years
1956 reset — the 7th Constitutional Amendment Act abolished the Part A, B and C state
classifications; the original Article 371 was replaced to provide development boards for Maharashtra
and Gujarat
Subsequent additions — Articles 371A to 371J were added incrementally to manage ethnic rebellions,
tribal land rights, border statehood transitions and regional educational and employment
disparities
Important Facts for Exams
Part XXI — Temporary, Transitional and Special Provisions; Articles 369 to 392
Article 370 related to Jammu and Kashmir; abrogated in 2019
The 7th Amendment (1956) abolished the Part A, B, C state classification and rewrote Article 371
371A (Nagaland) and 371G (Mizoram) are the strongest — central laws on land and custom need
Assembly concurrence
371A arose from the 16-Point Agreement, 1960; 371G from the Mizo Peace Accord, 1986
371D/E arose from the Six-Point Formula for Andhra Pradesh
371J covers Kalyana-Karnataka, added by the 98th Amendment, 2012
Proposed 371K — a directly elected UT-level legislative body for Ladakh, without statehood or Sixth
Schedule status
Sixth Schedule applies to tribal areas of Assam, Meghalaya, Tripura and Mizoram, through
Autonomous District Councils

LAHDC Leh (1995) and LAHDC Kargil (2003) operate under the LAHDC Act, 1997
2. Hazard Perception Test
Source: The Hindu
Context Kerala will become the first State in India to introduce a mandatory computer-based Hazard
Perception Test (HPT) for driving licence applicants, from December 2026.
What It Is
A computer-based road-safety assessment
Uses simulated traffic situations to test whether applicants can identify potential hazards at the
appropriate time
The provision for such an assessment already exists under the Central Motor Vehicles Rules
Aim
Move driver testing beyond knowledge of traffic rules and vehicle handling
Toward real-world hazard recognition, anticipation and safer decision-making
Important Facts for Exams
Kerala — the first State to make the Hazard Perception Test mandatory, from December 2026
Provision already available under the Central Motor Vehicles Rules, 1989
The test measures anticipation, not rule knowledge — the gap between knowing a rule and reacting
in time
The UK pioneered the HPT in 2002, as part of its theory test; Australia and several other countries also
use it
Driving licences in India are issued by State Transport Authorities / RTOs under the Motor Vehicles
Act, 1988
Motor vehicles is a Concurrent List subject
Road accident data 2024: 4,87,707 accidents and 1,77,175 deaths; about 485 deaths daily
Accident severity rose from 21.6 (2005) to 36.3 (2024) deaths per 100 accidents
Two-wheeler riders 46.2 per cent and pedestrians 20.6 per cent of fatalities
18–45 age group: 66.1 per cent of deaths; estimated 3 per cent GDP loss
Related: the Motor Vehicles (Amendment) Act, 2019, which introduced electronic enforcement
(136A), Good Samaritan protection (134A), Golden Hour cashless treatment (162), contractor liability
(198A) and vehicle recall (110A)
Also related: automated driving test tracks, Bharat NCAP vehicle safety ratings, and the Safe
Systems approach
India is a signatory to the Brasilia Declaration and committed to SDG 3.6 — halving road traffic deaths

Agriculture
1. FAO Report: "Forests for Food"
Source: Down To Earth
Context The FAO, with the Landscape Alliance (CIFOR-ICRAF), published "Forests for Food: Nourishing
People, Sustaining the Planet" — developed under the Collaborative Partnership on Forests (CPF) Joint
Initiative.
What It Is
Synthesises global evidence on the links between forests and the four dimensions of Food Security
and Nutrition — availability, access, utilisation and stability
Addresses a systemic knowledge gap caused by:
Absence of globally standardised definitions
Unrecorded informal wild-food consumption
Fragmented policy silos across agriculture, forestry and health
Key Findings
Over 5 billion people depend to some extent on forests for livelihoods; forests generate up to 20 per
cent of rural household income in several developing regions
More than 50,000 wild species are used globally for food, energy, medicine and materials — including
at least 10,000 consumed directly as human food
In remote forest-dependent communities, wild forest foods supply up to 80 per cent of daily dietary
protein and nearly half of total food mass by weight
1.8 billion people (23 per cent of the global population) relied primarily on woodfuel for cooking in
2022; 410 million depended on wood energy to boil and sterilise drinking water
Forests host pollinators supporting 35 per cent of global crop production; forested watersheds
supply freshwater to over 85 per cent of the world's major cities
Agricultural zones across 155 countries depend on forests in other countries for up to 40 per cent of
annual rainfall, through atmospheric moisture recycling and evapotranspiration
More than 1 billion people rely on forest-derived medicinal plants for primary healthcare
10.9 million hectares of forest were deforested annually between 2015 and 2025
Important Facts for Exams
Report: "Forests for Food: Nourishing People, Sustaining the Planet" — by FAO with CIFOR-ICRAF,
under the CPF Joint Initiative
FAO — headquartered at Rome, founded 1945; also publishes SOFA, SOFI and the Global Forest
Resources Assessment
CIFOR-ICRAF — Center for International Forestry Research and World Agroforestry

September 2026 full compilation  ·  Page 126 of 191

WhatsApp channel: whatsapp.com/channel/0029Vao03aUADTO9sOoigE2F  ·  +91 87086 52887  ·  c4scourses.in  ·  free, share it on
Four dimensions of food security: availability, access, utilisation, stability
5 billion people depend on forests; up to 20 per cent of rural income
50,000+ wild species used; 10,000+ eaten directly
80 per cent of protein in remote forest communities from wild foods
1.8 billion rely on woodfuel for cooking; 410 million for boiling water
Pollinators support 35 per cent of global crop production; forested watersheds supply 85 per cent of
major cities
155 countries depend on foreign forests for up to 40 per cent of rainfall

Arapaima — the Amazon giant fish, recovered 30-fold through community management
VGGT — FAO Voluntary Guidelines on Responsible Governance of Tenure
One Health — the integrated approach linking human, animal and environmental health
2. India's First Soil Carbon Payments
Source: PIB
Context India's agricultural carbon market reached a milestone as 2,550+ small farmers in Punjab and
Haryana received over ₹2.9 crore through DBT — India's first farm-level soil carbon payments for
verified regenerative farming practices.
What It Is
A digital market-linkage mechanism monetising verified soil organic carbon sequestration and
greenhouse gas reductions
Rewarding smallholder farmers for regenerative, climate-smart agronomic practices
Implementing Entity
Grow Indigo — an agri-tech joint venture between Mahyco and US-based Indigo Ag
Through its farmer-carbon initiative, the 'Aadi' programme
Background
Initiated in 2019 to curb air pollution from paddy stubble burning in northwestern India and reverse
groundwater depletion in the Indo-Gangetic plains
The first payment cycle covers carbon sequestered and emissions avoided between 2019 and 2022
Initial tranche of roughly 30,000 acres, generating over 50,000 internationally certified carbon
credits
How it Works?
Stage How It Works
1. Field Practice Adoption Farmers adopt Direct Seeded Rice (DSR), minimum tillage, cover crops, and
in-situ residue retention, replacing conventional transplanting and stubble
burning.
2. Measurement, Reporting &
Verification (MRV)
Soil carbon and GHG changes are tracked using satellite data, digital crop
models, and periodic soil sampling.
3. Credit Issuance & Monetisation Verified carbon credits are sold in the voluntary carbon market.
4. Farmer Payment Farmers receive upfront payments or 75% of the net proceeds from carbon-
credit sales.

1. The EPFO Wage Ceiling
Source: PIB
Context The Union Cabinet approved the Ministry of Labour and Employment's proposal to raise the
mandatory wage ceiling under the EPFO from ₹15,000 to ₹25,000 per month.
What the Wage Ceiling Is
The statutory monthly salary threshold — basic pay plus dearness allowance
Determining mandatory coverage under the EPF and Miscellaneous Provisions Act, 1952
Any worker joining an establishment with 20 or more employees at a wage up to this limit must be
mandatorily enrolled in the provident fund, pension and insurance schemes
Aim
Align statutory social security with sustained wage growth, rising cost of living and inflation
Ensure entry- and mid-level formal sector workers receive portable, assured retirement savings
and survivor benefits
The Revision History
₹6,500 per month from 2004 until 1 September 2014
Revised to ₹15,000 in 2014
Over the following 12 years, rising state minimum wages brought baseline earnings close to or above
₹15,000, leaving new workers outside automatic coverage

2. Moody's Raises India's FY27 GDP Forecast to 7%
Source: Business Standar
Context Moody's Ratings raised its forecast for India's real GDP growth in FY27 to 7 per cent from 6 per
cent — citing stronger-than-expected economic activity and the economy's resilience to the West
Asia conflict shock.
The Forecast Comparison
Agency FY27 GDP projection
Moody's 7.0 per cent
RBI 6.7 per cent
ADB 6.6 per cent
S&P Global 6.6 per cent
Goldman Sachs 6.5 per cent
Fitch 6.4 per cent
Moody's projection is the highest among major agencies.
What Drove the Upgrade
Real GDP growth accelerated to 8.2 per cent year-on-year in the first six months of 2026, against 7.3
per cent in 2025
Driven by strong private consumption, robust gross fixed capital formation and sustained services
activity
Continued public infrastructure spending and a likely revival in private-sector investment are
supporting growth
Important Facts for Exams
Moody's raised FY27 India GDP forecast from 6 per cent to 7 per cent — the highest among major
agencies
RBI 6.7, ADB 6.6, S&P 6.6, Goldman Sachs 6.5, Fitch 6.4
Real GDP grew 8.2 per cent in the first half of 2026, against 7.3 per cent in 2025
Rating: Baa3, stable outlook — Moody's lowest investment grade
Equivalent ratings: Fitch BBB−, S&P BBB, Morningstar DBRS BBB — all stable
S&P upgraded India to BBB from BBB− last year, one notch above Fitch and Moody's
Constraints: high general government debt, weak debt affordability, low per capita income
Moody's FY27 inflation projection 4.8 per cent, against 2.4 per cent in FY26
Debt affordability — the share of revenue consumed by interest payments; India's is among the
weakest in its rating category
General government debt includes Centre plus states; S&P projects the combined deficit at 7.3 per

Agriculture
1. SPROUT Facility Inaugurated at BRIC-NIPGR
Source: PIB
Context Union Minister of State (IC), Science and Technology, Dr Jitendra Singh inaugurated SPROUT — a
state-of-the-art agricultural research facility at the BRIC-National Institute of Plant Genome Research
(NIPGR), New Delhi — to develop climate-resilient crops.
What SPROUT Stands For
SpeedSeed Phenotyping and Resource Optimization for Unified Trait Analysis
It is an agricultural technology platform focused on rapid and efficient analysis of crop traits.
Speed Seed Phenotyping: Quickly studies and measures important characteristics (traits) of seeds
and plants.
Resource Optimization: Helps reduce the time, labour and resources needed for crop evaluation.
Unified Trait Analysis: Brings different crop-trait measurements together for more systematic
analysis.
Aim
Develop climate-resilient crops by enabling precise trait evaluation under varied environmental
conditions
Strengthen plant genome research and high-throughput phenotyping
Important Facts for Exams
Located at BRIC-NIPGR, New Delhi; Director Prof. Manoj Prasad
Initial crop: chickpea
SpeedSeed / speed breeding cuts crop cycles to about 40 days, allowing several generations per
year instead of one or two
Phenotyping — measuring a plant's observable traits (height, yield, stress response); high-
throughput means automated, at large scale
Genotype vs phenotype — genotype is the genetic makeup; phenotype is the expressed traits, shaped
by genes and environment
BRIC — Biotechnology Research and Innovation Council, an autonomous body under the
Department of Biotechnology, formed 2023 by merging 14 DBT institutes
NIPGR — established 1998; focuses on plant genomics
BioE3 Policy — approved 2024, promoting biomanufacturing and a high-performance bioeconomy
India's first genome-edited rice varieties — DRR Dhan 100 (Kamala) and Pusa DST Rice 1 — were
released in 2025

Banking and Finance
1. NPCI Revises UPI MDR Framework
NPCI released a revised Merchant Discount Rate (MDR) framework, effective from 15 October 2026.
P2P UPI transactions will remain free, irrespective of the transaction amount.
UPI P2M transactions below ₹2,000 will continue to remain free for merchants and users.
For specified categories such as railways, telecom, insurance and fuel, a flat ₹5 MDR will apply to
transactions above ₹2,000; capital-market transactions will attract 0.02%, capped at ₹300.
2. NSE Enables India’s First Tokenised Corporate Bond Issuances
NSE enabled India’s first tokenised corporate bond issuances, worth ₹1,000 crore, under the SEBI
Regulatory Sandbox Framework, 2020.
REC and L&T each raised ₹500 crore through tokenised corporate bonds.
The issuances use Distributed Ledger Technology (DLT) to digitally represent and manage securities.
The technology aims to improve transparency, operational efficiency and settlement processes.

durable liquidity.
It is the first tranche of a ₹1 trillion programme, with ₹25,000 crore auctions scheduled for 21 and 28
September 2026.

What does the IEMI measure?
The India Electric Mobility Index evaluates EV progress through three broad areas:
Transport Electrification Progress – extent of electric vehicle adoption and electrification of transport.
Charging Infrastructure Readiness – availability of charging infrastructure and supporting measures.
EV Research and Innovation Readiness – development of research, innovation and the EV startup
ecosystem.

Social Issue
1. Registration of Births and Deaths Amendment Act, 2026
The Registration of Births and Deaths (Amendment) Act, 2026 will come into force from 1 October
2026.
Births and deaths must generally be reported within 21 days of occurrence.
Delayed registration after one year and up to two years will require approval from the District
Magistrate, SDM or authorised Executive Magistrate.
Registration delayed by more than two years will require an order from the Judicial Magistrate First
Class (JMFC).

Reports & Articles
1. WMO State of Global Water Resources 2025
Source: WMO
Context The World Meteorological Organization's State of Global Water Resources 2025 report warned
of persistent groundwater depletion in India, particularly in north-western regions.
What the Report Is
An annual global assessment by the WMO tracking the state, availability and variability of freshwater
resources
Expanded over five years from 3 to 15 hydrological variables — covering groundwater, river
discharge, terrestrial water storage, glaciers, soil moisture and other water cycle components
Key Findings
Groundwater stress — in 2025, 34 per cent of monitored groundwater stations recorded below to
much-below-normal levels, up from 25 per cent in 2024
India's persistent depletion — parts of India saw persistent groundwater deficits during 2022–2025,
with over-abstraction an important driver
Declining freshwater storage — global terrestrial water storage has declined persistently since
2014–16; in 2025, below-normal storage persisted in several regions including northern India
Drying rivers — about 36 per cent of global catchment area was drier than normal, making 2025 one
of the driest years for global river discharge in 35 years
Accelerating glacier loss — glaciers lost 408 ±132 Gt of mass in 2025, equivalent to about 1.1 ±0.4 mm
of sea-level rise; the fourth consecutive year in which every major glaciated region recorded net ice
loss
Important Facts for Exams
WMO — World Meteorological Organization, a UN specialised agency, headquartered at Geneva,
founded 1950
34 per cent of groundwater stations below normal in 2025, up from 25 per cent
Terrestrial water storage declining since 2014–16
36 per cent of global catchment drier than normal; one of the driest years in 35 years
Terrestrial water storage — all water on and below the land surface; measured by satellite gravimetry
such as GRACE
India is the world's largest user of groundwater — more than the US and China combined
Groundwater supports about 60 per cent of irrigation and 85 per cent of rural drinking water in India
The Central Ground Water Board (CGWB) assesses groundwater; categories are Safe, Semi-critical,
Critical and Over-exploited

September 2026 full compilation  ·  Page 141 of 191
“Keep the streak. Momentum is the cheapest fuel there is.”
WhatsApp channel: whatsapp.com/channel/0029Vao03aUADTO9sOoigE2F  ·  +91 87086 52887  ·  c4scourses.in  ·  free, share it on

Banking and Finance
1. FCRA and the 2026 Amendment Bill
Source: PIB
Context The proposed Foreign Contribution (Regulation) Amendment Bill, 2026 has raised concerns
over tighter regulation of NGOs and provisions on assets created from foreign contributions when FCRA
registration ceases.
What FCRA Is
Regulates acceptance and utilisation of foreign contributions and foreign hospitality by individuals,
associations and organisations, including NGOs
Administered by the Ministry of Home Affairs
Objective: ensure foreign funds do not adversely affect India's sovereignty, security, public interest or
democratic processes
Core Provisions of the 2026 Bill
Asset vesting — if an NGO's registration is cancelled, surrendered or lapses, all unutilised foreign
contributions and assets created from foreign funds provisionally vest in a government-appointed
Designated Authority
Permanent confiscation — if registration is not restored within a prescribed window, assets
permanently vest with the Authority, which may transfer them to public departments or auction
them, crediting proceeds to the Consolidated Fund of India

Deemed cessation — automatic cessation of registration if renewal is denied, not submitted or

Rationalised penalties — maximum prison term for specific compliance violations reduced from five
years to one year

Agriculture
1. Agasthyamala Biosphere Reserve
Source: The Hindu
Context A comprehensive checklist documented 3,261 flowering plant species in the Agasthyamala
Biosphere Reserve.
Location and Status
Spans the southern extremity of the Western Ghats, straddling Kerala and Tamil Nadu
Area: about 3,500 sq km; highest point Agasthyarkoodam peak, 1,868 m
Notified by the Government of India in 2001
Inscribed in the UNESCO World Network of Biosphere Reserves under the Man and the Biosphere
Programme in 2016
One of South India's four biosphere reserves, with Nilgiri, Gulf of Mannar and Seshachalam Hills
Major Plant Families
The most species-rich families recorded are:
Plant Family Number of Taxa
Fabaceae 349
Rubiaceae 194
Poaceae 193
Orchidaceae 168
Important Facts for Exams
Biosphere Reserve: A protected area designed to conserve biodiversity while allowing sustainable use
of natural resources.
Endemic Species: A species naturally restricted to a particular geographical area.
Steno-endemic: A species or taxon restricted to a very small or specific geographical/habitat range.
Biogeography: Study of the geographical distribution of living organisms.
Angiosperms: Flowering plants that produce seeds enclosed within fruits.
Fabaceae: The legume/pea family, including beans, peas, lentils, chickpeas and many other plants.
Rubiaceae: The coffee family, which includes coffee, quinine and many tropical flowering plants.
Poaceae: The grass family, including rice, wheat, maize, sugarcane and bamboo.
Orchidaceae: The orchid family, known for its highly diverse and often ornamental flowering plants.
Western Ghats: A major mountain system along India's western coast and one of the world's
important biodiversity hotspots.

3. OMO Auction
Source: BS
Context The RBI conducted an OMO sale auction, offloading ₹25,000 crore of Government of India
securities to drain durable surplus rupee liquidity.
What an OMO Is
A quantitative monetary policy tool — outright purchases or sales of G-Secs and Treasury Bills in the
secondary market to regulate reserve money
Three Types
Outright OMO Sale — RBI sells G-Secs from its portfolio, permanently absorbing durable liquidity
Outright OMO Purchase — RBI buys G-Secs, injecting durable liquidity
Simultaneous Sale and Purchase (Operation Twist) — selling short-term and buying long-term
securities to flatten the yield curve without expanding the balance sheet
How It Works
Notification and e-Kuber bidding — eligible primary dealers, scheduled commercial banks and
institutions place electronic yield or price bids on the RBI's e-Kuber platform
Allocation — bids accepted up to notified cut-off yields, by multiple or uniform price auction
Liquidity drain — banks transfer funds from their current accounts with the RBI, locking reserve cash

September 2026 full compilation  ·  Page 151 of 191
“Cover the answer, say it aloud, then check. Every time.”
WhatsApp channel: whatsapp.com/channel/0029Vao03aUADTO9sOoigE2F  ·  +91 87086 52887  ·  c4scourses.in  ·  free, share it on
into interest-earning G-Secs
Key Features
Direct quantitative balance-sheet instrument — unlike SDF or MSF, which address short-term needs
Discretionary flexibility — the RBI can set security-wise limits, reject mispriced bids and vary
accepted amounts
Phased execution — large operations split into tranches to avoid sudden bond-yield disruption
Important Facts for Exams
Current programme: ₹1 trillion in three tranches — ₹50,000 cr (17 Sept), ₹25,000 cr (21 Sept), ₹25,000
cr (28 Sept)
e-Kuber — the RBI's core banking solution, used for auctions and government transactions
Operation Twist — simultaneous sale and purchase to reshape the yield curve
DvP — Delivery versus Payment settlement
Liquidity tools: VRRR (transient); OMO sales, CRR, sell/buy swaps, MSS (durable)
Durable liquidity: ₹10.66 trillion (31 August), estimated near ₹14 trillion by mid-September
WACR is the operating target; repo 5.25 per cent, SDF 5.00 per cent

What It Is
A non-superpower multilateral coalition of middle powers, regional organisations and developing

September 2026 full compilation  ·  Page 153 of 191
“Cover the answer, say it aloud, then check. Every time.”

Origin

Authentication can be completed through delivery personnel, LPG distributor showrooms or OMC
mobile applications.
Consumers who do not complete authentication can still obtain LPG through specified channels, but

The measure is linked to the authentication of beneficiaries receiving the implicit subsidy on
domestic LPG.

1. Global Business Climate Survey 2026
Source: BS
Context India was ranked first in business climate among 41 global markets in the Global Business
Climate Survey 2026, reflecting strong confidence among Swedish companies operating in India.
What the Survey Is
An annual international benchmark capturing the operational perspectives, performance,
profitability and expansion plans of Swedish multinational corporations operating in key global
markets
First instituted in 2020; the 2026 edition is the largest to date
Who Conducts It
Business Sweden
The Swedish Chambers of Commerce (SCI)
Swedish embassies and consulates
In partnership with the Swedish Chamber of Commerce in India (SCCI)
Aim
Provide a comparative framework tracking macroeconomic conditions, regulatory hurdles, ease of
doing business and forward-looking investment sentiment for Swedish enterprises abroad
The Top Three
India — first
Ireland — second
Vietnam — third
Standing out for economic momentum, political stability and high investor confidence.
India's Performance
Ranked No. 1 overall in business climate favourability
In the global top three for four consecutive years
73 per cent of surveyed Swedish companies rated India's operating climate good or very good
Along with the Philippines and Poland, India recorded the highest market-level revenue and growth
projections
Global Findings
Despite geopolitical shocks, 64 per cent of Swedish companies globally reported profitability
80 per cent maintained a neutral or positive operational outlook

2. Neelakurinji
Source: The Hindu
Context Rampant destruction of Neelakurinji blooms across unprotected hill ranges in Munnar —
including Lakshmi Hills and Chinnakkanal — triggered serious concern among Kerala Forest
Department officials.
What It Is
A purplish-blue flowering shrub of the genus Strobilanthes, family Acanthaceae
The most famous species is Strobilanthes kunthiana
Renowned for synchronised mass gregarious flowering every 12 years
Habitat and Distribution
Exclusively adapted to montane shola-grassland ecosystems of peninsular India
Typically at 1,000 to 2,500 metres above sea level
Kerala — Munnar, Eravikulam National Park
Tamil Nadu — Nilgiri Hills, Palani Hills
Karnataka — Mandalpatti (Kodagu), Baba Budangiri and Mullayanagiri (Chikkamagaluru)
Kurinjimala Sanctuary was notified by Kerala specifically to protect its habitat
Key Characteristics
Plietesial lifecycle and mast seeding — flowers synchronously after long vegetative cycles of 3 to 16
years (12 years for S. kunthiana), sets seed and dies
Shrub morphology — erect, 0.5 to 3 metres; some species up to 8 metres; opposite, stalked leaves
containing mineral deposits called cystoliths
Tubular hooded inflorescence — two-lipped flowers in dense spikes or panicles, in purplish-blue,
violet and pale pink
Pollination and honey — mass flowering attracts Indian honeybees and giant rock bees, producing
Kurinjithen, a dark honey valued for medicinal properties
Statutory protection — S. kunthiana is listed under Schedule III of the Wildlife (Protection) Act, 1972;
unlawful collection or destruction may attract imprisonment and fines
Important Facts for Exams
Strobilanthes kunthiana, family Acanthaceae; blooms once in 12 years
Genus: about 450 species globally, 160+ in India, 72+ endemic to the Western Ghats
Kurinjimala Sanctuary (Kerala) protects its habitat; Eravikulam National Park is the other key site
The Nilgiris ("blue mountains") are named after it
Added to Schedule III, Wildlife (Protection) Act, 1972 in 2024
Plietesial — grows vegetatively for years, flowers en masse once, sets seed and dies
Mast seeding — synchronous mass seed production, which overwhelms seed predators
Cystolith — a calcium carbonate deposit in leaf cells, characteristic of Acanthaceae
Kurinjithen — the honey produced from Neelakurinji nectar

1. RBI Bulletin: State of the Economy" Report
Source: Times of India
Context The RBI Bulletin's "State of the Economy" report said the Indian economy has performed well
despite global headwinds, while noting that the escalation in West Asia this month caused a sharp rise
in oil prices, reviving fears of inflationary pressure and supply chain disruption.
Key Highlights
GDP Growth: India's economy recorded strong growth in Q1 2026–27.
Global Risks: Geopolitical tensions, higher crude oil prices and weather uncertainty remain key risks.
FCNR(B) Deposits: Banks mobilised about $13.3 billion through FCNR(B) deposits, supporting liquidity
and the rupee initially.
Bank Deposits: Bank deposits recorded their fastest growth in 15 years in August.
FDI: Net FDI rose 64% to $7.3 billion in July 2026.
Major FDI Sectors: Communication, financial services and computer services.
Major FDI Sources: Mauritius, UAE and the USA.
Important Facts for Exams
FCNR(B): Foreign Currency Non-Resident (Bank) deposits held by eligible NRIs in foreign currencies.
FDI: Investment by a foreign entity in a business in another country.
Global Headwinds: Global conditions that create challenges for economic growth.
Top source countries: Mauritius, UAE, US — about 70 per cent of equity inflows
Top sectors: communication, financial and computer services — over four-fifths
Net FDI = gross inflows − repatriation/disinvestment − outward FDI by Indian firms
Mauritius dominates as a source because of tax treaty and routing history — the recorded source is
often not the true origin of funds
FDI is a non-debt creating inflow; FCNR(B) deposits are debt-creating

6. RBI Final Directions on Market Risk Capital and Eased FPI KYC

2026 under Basel III.

Review of the Trading Book (FRTB) framework.
RBI also eased KYC requirements for Foreign Portfolio Investors (FPIs) from 18 September 2026,
allowing specified documents to be certified by authorised authorities outside India.
Market risk refers to possible losses due to changes in interest rates, equity prices, exchange rates
and commodity prices.

RBI  AND  NABARD  CURRENT  AFFAIRS
Social Issue
1. The Removal of the Chief Election Commissioner
Source: Indian Express
Context A coalition of Opposition parties announced plans to submit notices of motion in both Houses
of Parliament seeking the removal of CEC Gyanesh Kumar.
Key Highlights
Article 324(5): The CEC can be removed only in the same manner and on the same grounds as a
Supreme Court Judge.
Grounds: Removal is possible only for proved misbehaviour or incapacity.
Other Election Commissioners: They can be removed by the President only on the recommendation
of the CEC.
Notice: Requires at least 100 Lok Sabha MPs or 50 Rajya Sabha MPs.
Inquiry Committee:
Supreme Court Judge1.
Chief Justice of a High Court2.
Distinguished jurist3.
Parliamentary approval: The motion must pass in both Houses by a special majority:
Majority of the total membership of the House, and
At least two-thirds of members present and voting.
Final step: After Parliament passes the address, it is presented to the President, who issues the
removal order.
Judges (Inquiry) Act, 1968: Provides the framework for the inquiry process.
Important Facts for Exams
Article 324(5) — CEC removal only like a Supreme Court judge; ECs only on the CEC's
recommendation
Article 124(4) — grounds: proved misbehaviour or incapacity; requires a special majority
Judges (Inquiry) Act, 1968 governs the inquiry
Special majority under Article 124(4) — majority of total membership plus two-thirds of those
present and voting
No CEC has ever been removed; no Supreme Court or High Court judge has ever been removed by
this route either — Justice V. Ramaswami (1993) came closest, when the motion failed for want of the
required majority in the Lok Sabha
The weaker protection for ECs — removable on the CEC's recommendation — has long been criticised,
and was noted in Anoop Baranwal (2023)
Tenure: six years or age 65, whichever is earlier; no reappointment

2. UN Gender Snapshot 2026
Source: UN Women
Context UN Women and UN DESA released "Progress on the Sustainable Development Goals: The
Gender Snapshot 2026" on 17 September 2026 — drawing on more than 100 data sources to track
progress for women and girls across all 17 SDGs.
Key Highlights
Gender Institutions: Around 1 in 3 gender-equality institutions have been weakened; 1 in 4 faced
budget cuts.
Funding: Between 2023–24, bilateral aid for gender equality fell 13%, while funding for women's rights
organisations fell 28%.
UN Efforts: 42% of UN entities reported weakened gender-equality efforts due to funding cuts.
Political Representation:
6 in 7 countries are led by men.
Nearly 90% of defence ministers are men.
More than 80% of finance ministers are men.
National Parliaments: Women's representation increased from 22.3% (2015) to 27.4% (2026).
Local Government: Women hold 35.6% of positions, with little change since 2023.
India-Specific Facts
Global Gender Gap Index 2026: India ranked 131st, with 64.5% parity.
Lok Sabha: Women constitute about 14% of members.
Women's Reservation: The 106th Constitutional Amendment Act, 2023 provides 33% reservation for
women in the Lok Sabha and State Assemblies, subject to delimitation following the relevant census.
Panchayats: At least one-third of seats are reserved for women under Articles 243D and 243T; some
states provide 50% reservation.
Female Labour Force Participation Rate: 41.7% in 2023–24.
Key Terms
SDG 5: Gender Equality — aims to achieve gender equality and empower all women and girls.
UN Women: UN entity dedicated to gender equality and women's empowerment, established in 2010.
UN DESA: UN Department of Economic and Social Affairs, involved in monitoring and reporting on the
SDGs.

5. The CEC and Other Election Commissioners Act, 2023
The Supreme Court delivered a split verdict on whether challenges to the Act should be heard by a
five-judge Constitution Bench; the matter has been referred accordingly.
The Act provides the statutory framework for the appointment, qualifications, service conditions and
tenure of the Chief Election Commissioner (CEC) and Election Commissioners (ECs).
It replaced the Election Commission (Conditions of Service of Election Commissioners and
Transaction of Business) Act, 1991.
The Anoop Baranwal judgment (2023) had provided an interim selection committee comprising the
Prime Minister, Lok Sabha Leader of Opposition and Chief Justice of India until Parliament made a
law.

12. Exercise VARUNA
VARUNA is the annual bilateral naval exercise between the Indian Navy and French Navy.
The 24th edition is being hosted at Toulon Naval Base, France, with INS Trishul representing India.
The exercise began as India-France naval interactions in 1983 and was formally named VARUNA in
2001.
Major activities include air defence, anti-submarine warfare, surface combat, tactical drills,
replenishment and coordinated manoeuvres. Other major India-France exercises are Shakti (Army)
and Garuda (Air Force).
13. Polylactic Acid (PLA)-Based Biodegradable Bioplastics
PLA is a bio-based and compostable thermoplastic produced by converting plant-based sugars into
lactic acid, which is then polymerised.
Feedstocks include sugarcane, molasses, corn starch and cassava, creating a potential waste-to-
wealth model using agricultural resources.
A 100 TPA pilot facility is being developed at Kumbhi, Uttar Pradesh, to test the technology and
develop specialised PLA grades.
PLA can be used in packaging, agricultural films, textiles and medical sutures, offering an alternative
to petroleum-based plastics.

Source: NABARD
Context NABARD launched RSVC-AMRIT, a digital platform for technology-led rural development, in

RSVC-AMRIT
SVC-AMRIT is a digital platform developed by NABARD in collaboration with the Office of the Principal
Scientific Adviser (OPSA), Government of India, to promote rural innovation and technology transfer. It
was launched on 15 August 2026 by Principal Scientific Adviser Prof. Ajay Kumar Sood.
AMRIT = Accelerated Medium for Rural Innovations and Technologies.
RSVC = RuTAGe Smart Village Centre
What It Does
Connects rural needs → relevant innovations and technologies → implementation partners
Aims to identify suitable technological solutions for challenges faced in rural areas
Helps connect innovators and technology providers with rural development requirements
Promotes the use of science, technology and innovation in rural development
Important Facts for Exams

Principal Scientific Adviser to the Government of India: Ajay Kumar Sood
The OPSA also anchors the BRICS BioDesign Programme and the Science and Technology Cluster
initiative
India Stack — the layered digital public infrastructure: identity (Aadhaar), payments (UPI) and data
(DEPA/Account Aggregator)

2. Livestock Insurance Portal
The Department of Animal Husbandry and Dairying launched the Livestock Insurance Portal as part
of the digital transformation of livestock insurance.
The portal is aimed at making livestock-insurance processes more accessible and efficient.
It is relevant to livestock farmers, who face financial losses from the death or loss of insured animals.
Livestock insurance is an important tool for reducing the financial risk associated with animal
husbandry.
3. Dhanuka Agritech Launches ‘Polin’ Fungicide for Paddy
Dhanuka Agritech Ltd launched ‘Polin’, a fungicide for protecting rice/paddy crops against leaf blast
and neck blast diseases.
The product has been developed through a strategic technical collaboration with Japan’s Nippon
Soda Co. Ltd. (NISSO).
It has translaminar and systemic mobility, allowing the fungicide to move through plant tissues and
protect unsprayed leaf areas and new plant growth.
The recommended application mentioned is 100 ml per acre in 200 litres of water, with application at
the first signs of infection during critical crop-growth stages.
Its longer protection period can reduce the frequency of spraying, potentially lowering labour and
chemical costs for rice farmers.
4. NABARD Opens ‘Experience Centre’ in Mumbai
NABARD inaugurated an Experience Centre at its Head Office in Mumbai.
The centre showcases NABARD's journey, major milestones, rural-development initiatives and
success stories.
Interactive exhibits allow visitors to explore NABARD-supported projects, schemes and interventions.
Key themes include climate-resilient agriculture, Self-Help Groups (SHGs), rural infrastructure and
digital financial inclusion.

Banking and Finance
1. The SWIFT Payment System
Source: The Hindu
Context Amid the weaponisation of the US dollar and Western financial sanctions, BRICS and Global

September 2026 full compilation  ·  Page 171 of 191
“Attempt what you know first. Speed follows accuracy.”
WhatsApp channel: whatsapp.com/channel/0029Vao03aUADTO9sOoigE2F  ·  +91 87086 52887  ·  c4scourses.in  ·  free, share it on
South nations are accelerating efforts to bypass the Belgium-based SWIFT messaging network.
What SWIFT Is
Society for Worldwide Interbank Financial Telecommunication
A standardised, secure messaging network used by financial institutions to transmit instructions and
information, such as money transfers, via standardised SWIFT/BIC codes
It is a messaging platform — not a settlement system or a bank
Founded 1973, headquartered at La Hulpe, Belgium
A member-owned cooperative under Belgian law, collectively supervised by the G10 central banks
Key Features
Pure messaging architecture — transmits payment orders, letters of credit and securities instructions;
the actual movement of money relies on correspondent banking accounts (Nostro/Vostro)
Standardised protocol — 8- or 11-character Business Identifier Codes (BIC) and the ISO 20022
messaging framework
Belgian and EU jurisdiction — obligating compliance with EU sanctions
Security — end-to-end hardware encryption, dedicated secure lines and geographically distributed
operations centres
The Alternatives
CIPS (China) — launched 2015 to internationalise the yuan; enables cross-border RMB messaging,
clearing and settlement across 120+ countries
SPFS (Russia) — developed 2014 after Western sanctions; connects Russian banks with foreign
institutions, including Iran's SEPAM
Project mBridge — a blockchain-based multi-CBDC platform developed by participating central
banks, enabling direct cross-border settlement using CBDCs, bypassing SWIFT and correspondent
banking
2. RBI Net Purchases Record $18.65 Billion in July
The RBI net purchased $18.65 billion in foreign exchange in July 2026, the highest monthly purchase
on record.
This was sharply higher than the $561 million net purchase in June 2026 and slightly above the
previous record of $18.63 billion in June 2021.
The increase was linked to the RBI's concessional swap facility for FCNR(B) deposits, external
commercial borrowings and overseas foreign-currency borrowings.
The facility attracted $40.82 billion of inflows by July-end, with banks routing the foreign currency
back to the RBI through the swap window.

1. The West Bengal Anti-Goonda Bill, 2026
The West Bengal Public Safety and Control of Anti-Social Activities Bill, 2026 was passed by the West
Bengal Assembly in June 2026.
Reports said the President returned the Bill for reconsideration after the Centre raised concerns
about possible overlap with the NDPS Act, 1985 and the PITNDPS Act, 1988.
The Bill deals with habitual offenders, organised crime and threats to public order, and includes
provisions for preventive detention.
There was subsequently a conflicting public statement from West Bengal Chief Minister Suvendu
Adhikari, who said the Bill was still under consideration of the Union Home Ministry. Therefore, the
procedural status should be treated with care.

components are replaced with vegetable oils, starches or plant proteins.
Common ingredients may include vegetable fats, skimmed milk powder, soy proteins, caseinates,
starches, emulsifiers and stabilisers.

5. Typhoon Dujuan
Typhoon Dujuan affected Tokyo and eastern Japan, bringing heavy rainfall and strong winds.
Izu Oshima Island recorded 832 mm of rainfall in 48 hours, while parts of Tokyo also experienced
record rainfall.
It was a tropical cyclone in the Northwestern Pacific, with reported maximum sustained winds of
around 130 km/h.
The name Dujuan, contributed by China, refers to the azalea flower.
6. Pandit Deendayal Upadhyaya

Sangh.
He served as its General Secretary from 1951 to 1967 and became its President in December 1967.
Integral Humanism emphasises balanced development of the body, mind, intellect and soul, along
with harmony between society and nature.
Antyodaya refers to the idea of ensuring that development reaches the poorest and most
marginalised sections.

What is Pashudhan Bima Portal?

through a single online system.

Insurance policy issuance
Online insurance applications
Claim processing
Real-time monitoring
Reporting
Data-driven management
What It Does

Facilitates better coordination, real-time monitoring and data-driven management of livestock
insurance programmes
Available in 11 regional languages, besides Hindi and English

Development Programme of the Department

cent for UTs
India is the world's largest milk producer, contributing about 24 per cent of global output

landless farmers

traceability and scheme delivery

2. The UN Declaration on Sea Level Rise
Source: UN News
Context At the 81st UN General Assembly, world leaders adopted the UN Declaration on Sea Level Rise
by consensus — addressing threats to the sovereignty, legal rights and survival of low-lying coastal
nations and Small Island Developing States (SIDS).
What It Is
A consensus-based international political declaration
Establishing global principles for safeguarding continued statehood, permanent maritime
entitlements and human rights of nations facing inundation
Adopted under UNGA Decision 78/558, co-facilitated by Australia and Cabo Verde
The Five Key Features
Presumption of continued statehood — sovereign statehood, legal personality and UN membership
remain intact even if territory is submerged
Freezing of maritime baselines — lawfully deposited UNCLOS maritime boundaries remain fixed and
permanent, with no requirement to revise them as coastlines retreat
Protection of affected persons — populations displaced across borders retain protections under
international human rights law, preserving nationality, dignity and the right to self-determination
Closing the adaptation finance gap — urging predictable, simplified climate adaptation funds for
vulnerable island nations
Scientific observation and early warnings — oceanic data sharing, coastal risk mapping and multi-
hazard early warning systems
Key Terms
Sea-Level Rise: Long-term increase in the average level of the world's oceans, mainly due to global
warming and melting ice.
SIDS: Small Island Developing States that are particularly vulnerable to climate change, sea-level rise
and extreme weather events.
UNCLOS: United Nations Convention on the Law of the Sea, the major international legal framework
governing oceans and maritime zones.
Maritime Entitlements: Rights of coastal states over maritime areas such as the territorial sea,
contiguous zone, EEZ and continental shelf.
JICA-Supported Effective Forest Management Project
The Ministry of Environment, Forest and Climate Change (MoEFCC) launched the Capacity
Enhancement for Effective Forest Management Project with support from JICA.
The project will run from 2025–26 to 2031–32 and focuses on improving the capacity of frontline forest
personnel.
It covers sustainable forest management, wildlife management, climate challenges, training and
research.
The project has a total outlay of ₹537.10 crore, including ₹440.42 crore from JICA and ₹96.68 crore
from the Government of India.

OECD Raises India's FY27 GDP Growth Forecast to 7.1%
The OECD Economic Outlook, Interim Report September 2026 raised India's FY27 GDP growth forecast
to 7.1%, from 6.3% projected earlier.
The upward revision was linked to strong domestic demand and government measures that
protected households and firms from higher energy prices.
India's growth is projected to moderate to 6.5% in FY28.
India's inflation is projected at 4.7% in FY27 and 4.2% in FY28.

Misc GA
PRAHAAR: National Counter-Terrorism Policy and Strategy
Source: News on Air
Context Union Home Minister Amit Shah inaugurated the Conference on 'PRAHAAR – National CT Policy
and Strategy' in New Delhi, and launched a new Extradition Portal.
The Seven Pillars of PRAHAAR
P — Prevention
R — Response
A — Aggregating Internal Capacities
H — Human Rights
A — Attenuating Conditions for Terrorism
A — Aligning International Efforts
R — Recovery and Resilience
Deliberated through a whole-of-society approach.
What is PRAHAAR?
PRAHAAR is India's national framework for dealing with terrorist activities, radicalization and the
wider terror ecosystem.
Its approach is intelligence-led and proactive, aiming to move beyond a purely reactive response to
terrorism. It also emphasises human rights, rule of law, technology, inter-agency coordination and
international cooperation.
Important Facts for Exams
PRAHAAR — the National Counter-Terrorism Policy and Strategy; seven pillars
288 fugitives brought back from 36 countries, 2019–2026
Shahzad Bhatti network — declared a terrorist organisation under the UAPA; 40+ modules busted,
350 arrests across 15 states

World Car-Free Day 2026
World Car-Free Day is observed on 22 September to encourage people to reduce private-car use and
promote sustainable transport.
The initiative developed from international efforts during the 1990s to reduce dependence on cars in
urban areas.
The first international Car-Free Day was held in 1999 as part of the European Union's “In Town Without
My Car” campaign.
It is associated with wider goals such as cleaner air, sustainable mobility and reduced urban
congestion.
International Day of Sign Languages 2026
The International Day of Sign Languages is observed on 23 September every year.
The 2026 theme is “Declaring Deaf People’s Human Rights.”
The UN designated 23 September as the day through Resolution A/RES/72/161 in 2017.
The date commemorates the establishment of the World Federation of the Deaf (WFD) in 1951.
National Service Scheme Day 2026
National Service Scheme (NSS) Day is observed on 24 September every year.
NSS was formally launched on 24 September 1969 in 37 universities.
It operates under the Ministry of Youth Affairs and Sports and focuses on student participation in
community service and nation-building activities.
The launch year, 1969, coincided with the birth centenary of Mahatma Gandhi.
World Heart Day 2026
World Heart Day is observed every year on 29 September.
It aims to create awareness about cardiovascular diseases, their prevention and their global impact.
The day promotes healthier lifestyles and greater awareness of heart-health risks.

India's First LNG-Powered Train
India launched its first LNG-powered passenger train from Sabarmati, Gujarat.
The 8-coach train uses a retrofitted LNG-diesel dual-fuel system, with LNG capable of replacing up
to 40% of diesel consumption during line-haul operations.
The inaugural route was Sabarmati–Mahesana on the Western Railway.
The initiative aims to reduce diesel use and emissions, particularly on routes where full electrification
is not yet available.

1. Afro-Asian Sand Snake Sighted in Kutch
Source: Indian Express
Context A sighting of the Afro-Asian Sand Snake was reported from Kutch, Gujarat — a species of
India's arid north-west.
What It Is
Psammophis schokari — also called the Schokari Sand Racer, Forskål's Sand Snake or Sind Sand
Snake
Family Psammophiidae (earlier placed under Lamprophiidae; often listed historically under
Colubridae)
First described by Forskål in 1775
Distribution
Found across North Africa, West Asia, Central Asia and north-western India — hence the name
"Afro-Asian"
In India, recorded from Gujarat, Rajasthan, Haryana, Punjab, Himachal Pradesh and Jammu &
Kashmir
One of about four Psammophis species recorded in India, alongside P. condanarus, P. leithii and P.
longifrons
Conservation Status
IUCN: Least Concern
Important Facts for Exams
Psammophis schokari — Afro-Asian Sand Snake / Schokari Sand Racer; family Psammophiidae
IUCN: Least Concern; described by Forskål, 1775
Distribution: North Africa to Central Asia and north-western India; in India from Gujarat, Rajasthan,
Punjab, Haryana, HP and J&K
Rear-fanged (opisthoglyphous) — fangs at the back of the upper jaw; venom is delivered by chewing,
and such snakes are generally not medically significant to humans
India's "Big Four" medically significant snakes: Russell's viper, saw-scaled viper, common krait and
spectacled cobra
Kutch, Gujarat — India's largest district, containing the Great and Little Rann of Kutch, a salt marsh
ecosystem
The Rann of Kutch is the world's only breeding ground for Greater Flamingos in the region —
Flamingo City

Source: PIB
Context India showcased its progress in forest and landscape restoration at the 28th Session of the FAO

What COFO Is
The highest statutory global intergovernmental body on forestry under the FAO
An international forum for policy dialogue, intergovernmental collaboration and technical direction
on forest ecosystems and landscape governance
Meets biennially at FAO Headquarters, Rome
Brings together 128 member countries, ministers, heads of national forest services and partner
agencies
Reports to the FAO Council on programme and budgetary matters, and to the FAO Conference on
global policy
Important Facts for Exams
COFO 28 — Rome; COFO is the highest FAO forestry body, meeting biennially, with 128 members
Publishes the State of the World's Forests (SOFO) — biennial — and the Global Forest Resources
Assessment (FRA) — five-yearly
India in FRA 2025: 9th in total forest area; 3rd in net annual gain; 82.74 mn ha, 25.17 per cent of
geographical area
India has 101 Ramsar Sites — the third-highest globally, after the UK and Mexico, and the most in Asia

DDU-GKY Completes 12 Years of Rural Skill Development
Deen Dayal Upadhyaya Grameen Kaushalya Yojana (DDU-GKY) was launched on 25 September
2014 under DAY-NRLM.
It provides placement-linked skill training to rural youth from poor households, mainly in the 15–35
years age group.
Since launch, over 18.47 lakh candidates have been trained and more than 12.43 lakh placed.
DDU-GKY 2.0, notified in May 2025, increased the minimum placement duration from 3 months to 6
months and also covers wage, self-employment and gig employment.

vulnerable groups.

RBI  CURRENT  AFFAIRS
Banking and Finance
1. What's Driving the Rupee's Depreciation
Source: Indian Express
Context The rupee has been under sustained pressure, driven by a combination of external shocks,
capital outflows and domestic macro imbalances — even as India's growth and reserves remain strong.
Key Figures
The rupee has depreciated about 8 per cent in real terms over the past year, per the REER
It touched an all-time low near ₹97 per dollar in May 2026

September 2026 full compilation  ·  Page 187 of 191
“The syllabus is finite. Your effort decides how soon it ends.”
WhatsApp channel: whatsapp.com/channel/0029Vao03aUADTO9sOoigE2F  ·  +91 87086 52887  ·  c4scourses.in  ·  free, share it on
Forex reserves reached a record $785.7 billion in the week ended 4 September
The RBI mobilised $136.37 billion through its concessional swap facility
Major Reasons Behind Rupee Depreciation
1. High Crude Oil Prices
India imports over 85% of its crude oil.
Brent crude crossed $100/barrel, nearing $110 amid West Asia tensions.
Higher oil prices increase dollar demand and import costs, putting pressure on the rupee.
Every $10 rise in crude prices may reduce India’s growth by about 44 basis points, according to Ind-
Ra.
2. Foreign Capital Outflows
Foreign investors selling Indian assets increase demand for foreign currency.
FPI equity outflows: about $19 billion in 2025 and $25 billion in 2026.
Around $13 billion flowed out in March 2026 alone.
Net FDI: only about $0.4 billion in 2024-25, partly due to investment repatriation.
3. Narrower India-US Interest Rate Differential
A smaller gap between Indian and US interest rates can reduce the attractiveness of rupee assets.
US rates were around 3.75–4%, while US Treasury yields also increased.
This can encourage investors to shift funds towards US assets, adding pressure on the rupee.
4. Structural Trade Deficit
India imports more goods than it exports, creating a persistent merchandise trade deficit.
Services exports and remittances partly offset this deficit.
CAD was around 0.6% of GDP in 2024-25 but may widen towards 2% of GDP.
Higher prices of energy, fertilisers and sulphur can further increase import costs.
Key Terms
Rupee Depreciation: Fall in the value of the rupee against foreign currencies, especially the US dollar.
FPI: Foreign Portfolio Investment in assets such as shares and bonds without controlling the
company.
FDI: Foreign Direct Investment involving long-term investment in a foreign business.
Trade Deficit: When a country’s imports of goods exceed its exports.
CAD: Current Account Deficit, when a country’s payments to the rest of the world exceed its receipts.
Interest Rate Differential: Difference between interest rates of two countries.
Terms of Trade: Ratio of a country’s export prices to import prices.
Basis Point (bp): 1 bp = 0.01 percentage point; 100 bps = 1 percentage point.

Nirmala Sitharaman launched the IITM Unicorn Frontier Fund-I, an Alternative Investment Fund
(AIF).
It is a joint initiative of Unicorn India Ventures, IIT Madras and IIT Madras Research Park.
The fund raised ₹450 crore in its first close, against a target of ₹1,000 crore.

1. Indian Delegation Attends SCO Meeting in Pakistan
Source: The Hindu
Context An Indian delegation led by a senior External Affairs Ministry official attended the SCO National
Coordinators' meeting in Islamabad — the first official Indian visit to Pakistan since Operation Sindoor.

About Shanghai Cooperation Organisation (SCO)
Established: 15 June 2001, at Shanghai
Founded by six countries: China, Kazakhstan, Kyrgyzstan, Russia, Tajikistan and Uzbekistan
Headquarters and Secretariat: Beijing, China
RATS — Regional Anti-Terrorist Structure: Tashkent, Uzbekistan
India and Pakistan joined: 2017
Current full members: 10
The Ten Members
China, India, Russia, Pakistan, Kazakhstan, Kyrgyzstan, Tajikistan, Uzbekistan, Iran and Belarus
Main Areas of Cooperation
Security — counter-terrorism, separatism and extremism, known as the "three evils"
Economy — trade, investment and economic cooperation
Connectivity — transport, infrastructure and regional connectivity
Energy — cooperation in energy-related areas
Culture and education — people-to-people cooperation
Important Facts for Exams
SCO National Coordinators' meeting, Islamabad; India represented by Joint Secretary Alok Dimri

Innovation and Shared Prosperity"

India and Pakistan joined in 2017; Iran 2023; Belarus 2024 — making 10 members

membership — meaning India could block it
